But I wouldn't say I feel sad.\n\nDoctor: Before you moved into the prototype-testing role, did you regularly have similar problems with attention or deciding what to do next?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Um, before I took that position, my focus was fine. I could get through my tasks and figure out what to do next without any issues. It really just started after the role change.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed increased difficulty remembering recent conversations or events?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My memory seems fine, honestly. I can remember conversations and what happened recently without any trouble. It's really just... getting stuck on what to do that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Has the move into the prototype-testing role felt significantly stressful to you?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, it's a new position, so there's always a little bit to learn, but I wouldn't say it's been significantly stressful. The work itself is fine, honestly. I just... I don't know, I just can't seem to get my brain to actually do it right now. But it's not because I feel overwhelmed or stressed out by the job itself.\n\nDoctor: When you are working, does your attention frequently wander away from the task even when you are trying to stay focused?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not like my mind wanders off to other things or I get distracted. I'll be looking right at the prototype or my screen, trying to work, but... I just can't get myself to take the next step. I'm focused on the task, it's just that nothing happens.\n\nDoctor: When you get stuck, do you understand what needs to be done but find yourself unable to start or choose the next step?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly it. It's actually why I came in, honestly... my brain just feels like it stalls out completely. I know what the task is, but I'll sit there with a test report open, or even just a simple CAD note, for an hour. I just can't decide what the next sentence should be, or which checkbox to click. I understand what needs to happen, but I just can't seem to choose that next step.\n\nDoctor: Once you begin a task, can you stay focused on it until it is finished?\n\nPatient: I mean, if I can actually get myself to start, it's a little better. I just sit there again, unable to make the next move to finish it.\n\nDoctor: Do you lose track of what you’re reading or what people are saying because your attention slips?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really lose track like that. It's not my attention slipping away. Like I was saying before, it's just that stalling feeling. I can read the words on the screen, or hear what someone is saying to me, but I just can't process what to do with it next. That's really the main reason I'm here. I'll just sit staring at that test report or the CAD note for an hour, knowing I need to write the next sentence, but my brain just won't let me decide what it should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble planning or organizing the steps of a task, even when you know what the overall goal is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, planning the steps is a big part of the problem. Like I mentioned, that's really why I came in... my brain just stalls out. I'll know the overall goal is to finish a test report or update a CAD note, but I can't figure out the sequence of what to do. I'll sit there for an hour just staring at it, knowing the end goal, but completely unable to organize or decide what the next sentence or the next checkbox should be to get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty switching from one task to another, even when you know what you need to do next?\n\nPatient: Yeah, switching is hard too, mostly because of that same issue I keep mentioning. I mean, it's a new position, so there's always a little bit to learn, but I wouldn't say it's been significantly stressful. The work itself is fine, honestly. I just... I don't know, I just can't seem to get my brain to actually do it right now. But it's not because I feel overwhelmed or stressed out by the job itself.\n\nDoctor: When you are working, does your attention frequently wander away from the task even when you are trying to stay focused?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not like my mind wanders off to other things or I get distracted. I'll be looking right at the prototype or my screen, trying to work, but... I just can't get myself to take the next step. I'm focused on the task, it's just that nothing happens.\n\nDoctor: When you get stuck, do you understand what needs to be done but find yourself unable to start or choose the next step?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly it. It's actually why I came in, honestly... my brain just feels like it stalls out completely. I know what the task is, but I'll sit there with a test report open, or even just a simple CAD note, for an hour. I just can't decide what the next sentence should be, or which checkbox to click. I understand what needs to happen, but I just can't seem to choose that next step.\n\nDoctor: Once you begin a task, can you stay focused on it until it is finished?\n\nPatient: I mean, if I can actually get myself to start, it's a little better. But... finishing it is still really hard. I don't get distracted by other things, but that same stuck feeling just comes back. I'll get halfway through a test procedure and then just stop. I just sit there again, unable to make the next move to finish it.\n\nDoctor: Do you lose track of what you’re reading or what people are saying because your attention slips?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really lose track like that. It's not my attention slipping away. Like I was saying before, it's just that stalling feeling. I can read the words on the screen, or hear what someone is saying to me, but I just can't process what to do with it next. That's really the main reason I'm here. I'll just sit staring at that test report or the CAD note for an hour, knowing I need to write the next sentence, but my brain just won't let me decide what it should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble planning or organizing the steps of a task, even when you know what the overall goal is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, planning the steps is a big part of the problem. Like I mentioned, that's really why I came in... my brain just stalls out. I'll know the overall goal is to finish a test report or update a CAD note, but I can't figure out the sequence of what to do. I'll sit there for an hour just staring at it, knowing the end goal, but completely unable to organize or decide what the next sentence or the next checkbox should be to get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty switching from one task to another, even when you know what you need to do next?\n\nPatient: Yeah, switching is hard too, mostly because of that same issue I keep mentioning. Like I said, that's really the whole reason I came in... my brain just completely stalls out. \n\nIf I try to move from one project to another, I just freeze. I'll switch over to a different test report, or open up a simple CAD note, and then I'll just sit there with it open for an hour. Even if I know I need to work on it next, I just can't decide what the next sentence should be or which checkbox to click to actually get going on it.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you have difficulty initiating or organizing ordinary daily activities, such as preparing a meal or completing a household task?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it happens at home, too. I know I keep sounding like a broken record about work, but that stalling feeling is exactly the same with normal stuff. \n\nI'll go into the kitchen to make dinner, and I just... I just stand there. I can't figure out what the first step should be. It's that same exact thing where my brain just completely stops, just like sitting in front of a test report for an hour unable to decide on a single sentence. So, yeah, it definitely affects me with household things, too.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you noticed that you have become less able to plan, organize, or make decisions than you were before?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my fiancé has definitely noticed. He's actually the one who finally told me I needed to see a doctor. He sees me just sitting there, staring at the computer screen or standing in the kitchen, and he knows something is wrong. I told him what I've been telling you—that my brain just stalls out. Like I said, I'll sit with a test report or a simple CAD note open for an hour and just can't decide what the next sentence or checkbox should be. He sees that happening all the time now, and he knows I used to be able to just get things done.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any formal cognitive testing, and if so, did it show a measurable impairment?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any kind of testing like that. This is actually the first time I'm seeing a doctor about any of this.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to manage your finances independently, as you did before these problems began?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I think I'm still okay with that. Paying bills and managing my bank account hasn't really been an issue. Most of my stuff is set up on autopay anyway, so it doesn't take a lot of, you know, active planning on my part. But yeah, I haven't noticed any problems with my finances.\n\nDoctor: When you understand information, is the main difficulty organizing, initiating, or choosing your next action, rather than understanding what you have read or heard?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say that's right.
